People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Most days food just doesn’t taste like much, so I’ll skip breakfast and just pick at dinner, even after being on a job site all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed being especially restless or slowed down. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed that happening. When I’m not weighed down or tired, I don’t think I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.

I used to look forward to fishing, working in the woodshop, playing chess with my kids… and now I just don’t care much. I haven’t been trying to, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. A lot, honestly. I keep feeling like I’ve let my wife and kids down, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—like a missed appointment, a cost overrun, anything with safety—I just take it as proof that I’m useless, like I shouldn’t be the one in charge. Sometimes it feels like everyone would be better off without me handling things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Around a year and seven months, I’d say. It started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already put in a full shift. Even just walking the job site or going up stairs with the crew feels heavy… like my body’s carrying wet concrete.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual most of the time?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m not totally sure if it’s most of the time. I do feel slowed down a lot, especially once the day gets going, but I don’t know if I notice it every moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not usually pacing or bouncing around. If anything, I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t noticed myself fidgeting much. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is… I’ve just stopped caring about things I used to really look forward to. My fishing gear’s been sitting untouched in the garage, I don’t want to go out to the woodshop anymore, and even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’m just kind of going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I know I haven’t been myself, and things feel pretty flat, but “sad most of the time”… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t really kept track.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. That’s kind of the main problem. I used to look forward to fishing, working in the woodshop, playing chess with my kids… and now I just don’t care much. It’s like I just don’t get much out of things anymore. Even being with my kids, like when they want to play chess, I’ll sit there and do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. Same with fishing or the woodshop—things I used to look forward to just feel like chores now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Most days food just doesn’t taste like much, so I’ll skip breakfast and just pick at dinner, even after being on a job site all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed being especially restless or slowed down. I mean, I feel worn out a lot, but as far as moving slower or not being able to sit still… I don’t know that I’ve paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Sometimes it feels like everyone would be better off without me handling things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Around a year and seven months, I’d say. It started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already put in a full shift. Even just walking the job site or going up stairs with the crew feels heavy… like my body’s carrying wet concrete.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual most of the time?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m not totally sure if it’s most of the time. I do feel slowed down a lot, especially once the day gets going, but I don’t know if I notice it every moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not usually pacing or bouncing around. If anything, I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t noticed myself fidgeting much. If anything I’m more just sitting still and feeling kind of weighed down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you're not feeling weighed down or tired?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed that happening. When I’m not weighed down or tired, I don’t think I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is… I’ve just stopped caring about things I used to really look forward to. My fishing gear’s been sitting untouched in the garage, I don’t want to go out to the woodshop anymore, and even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’m just kind of going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I know I haven’t been myself, and things feel pretty flat, but “sad most of the time”… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t really kept track.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. That’s kind of the main problem. I used to look forward to fishing, working in the woodshop, playing chess with my kids… and now I just don’t care much. The gear’s still sitting there, the shop feels like work, and with the kids I’m mostly just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Do you still get pleasure from things like eating or spending time with loved ones?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really. It’s like I just don’t get much out of things anymore. Even being with my kids, like when they want to play chess, I’ll sit there and do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. Same with fishing or the woodshop—things I used to look forward to just feel like chores now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Most days food just doesn’t taste like much, so I’ll skip breakfast and just pick at dinner, even after being on a job site all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed being especially restless or slowed down. I mean, I feel worn out a lot, but as far as moving slower or not being able to sit still… I don’t know that I’ve paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah. A lot, honestly. I keep feeling like I’ve let my wife and kids down, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—like a missed appointment, a cost overrun, anything with safety—I just take it as proof that I’m useless, like I shouldn’t be the one in charge. Sometimes it feels like everyone would be better off without me handling things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Especially on those early drives to work, before anyone else is awake. I’ll catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t make it there. And sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and a half… maybe a little more. Around a year and seven months, I’d say. It started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already put in a full shift. Even just walking the job site or going up stairs with the crew feels heavy… like my body’s carrying wet concrete.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual most of the time?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m not totally sure if it’s most of the time. I do feel slowed down a lot, especially once the day gets going, but I don’t know if I notice it every moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not usually pacing or bouncing around. If anything, I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t noticed myself fidgeting much. If anything I’m more just sitting still and feeling kind of weighed down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you're not feeling weighed down or tired?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed that happening. When I’m not weighed down or tired, I don’t think I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s been pretty steady for about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I can’t think of a couple months where I really felt better.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you are feeling your worst?\n\nPatient: When I’m at my worst, I’m usually not pacing around or anything like that. I mostly just feel stuck, like I’m sitting there and it’s hard to move. I haven’t really noticed fidgeting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. When I’m anxious or upset, I can feel tense, but I haven’t really noticed myself fidgeting a lot.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know I haven’t been myself, and things feel pretty flat, but “sad most of the time”… I don’t know. It’s like I just don’t get much out of things anymore. Even being with my kids, like when they want to play chess, I’ll sit there and do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. Same with fishing or the woodshop—things I used to look forward to just feel like chores now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Most days food just doesn’t taste like much, so I’ll skip breakfast and just pick at dinner, even after being on a job site all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed being especially restless or slowed down. I mean, I feel worn out a lot, but as far as moving slower or not being able to sit still… I don’t know that I’ve paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah. A lot, honestly. I keep feeling like I’ve let my wife and kids down, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—like a missed appointment, a cost overrun, anything with safety—I just take it as proof that I’m useless, like I shouldn’t be the one in charge. Sometimes it feels like everyone would be better off without me handling things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I’m not usually pacing or bouncing around. If anything, I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t noticed myself fidgeting much. If anything I’m more just sitting still and feeling kind of weighed down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you're not feeling weighed down or tired?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed that happening. When I’m not weighed down or tired, I don’t think I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s been pretty steady for about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I can’t think of a couple months where I really felt better.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you are feeling your worst?\n\nPatient: When I’m at my worst, I’m usually not pacing around or anything like that. I mostly just feel stuck, like I’m sitting there and it’s hard to move. I haven’t really noticed fidgeting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. When I’m anxious or upset, I can feel tense, but I haven’t really noticed myself fidgeting a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… like I said, not really. I used to look forward to fishing, working in the woodshop, playing chess with my kids… and now I just don’t care much. The gear’s still sitting there, the shop feels like work, and with the kids I’m mostly just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Do you still get pleasure from things like eating or spending time with loved ones?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really. It’s like I just don’t get much out of things anymore. Even being with my kids, like when they want to play chess, I’ll sit there and do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. Same with fishing or the woodshop—things I used to look forward to just feel like chores now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been trying to, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Most days food just doesn’t taste like much, so I’ll skip breakfast and just pick at dinner, even after being on a job site all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed being especially restless or slowed down. I mean, I feel worn out a lot, but as far as moving slower or not being able to sit still… I don’t know that I’ve paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah. A lot, honestly. I keep feeling like I’ve let my wife and kids down, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—like a missed appointment, a cost overrun, anything with safety—I just take it as proof that I’m useless, like I shouldn’t be the one in charge. Sometimes it feels like everyone would be better off without me handling things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Especially on those early drives to work, before anyone else is awake. I’ll catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t make it there. And sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and a half… maybe a little more. Around a year and seven months, I’d say. It started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already put in a full shift. Even just walking the job site or going up stairs with the crew feels heavy… like my body’s carrying wet concrete.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual most of the time?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m not totally sure if it’s most of the time. I do feel slowed down a lot, especially once the day gets going, but I don’t know if I notice it every moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not usually pacing or bouncing around. If anything, I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t noticed myself fidgeting much. If anything I’m more just sitting still and feeling kind of weighed down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you're not feeling weighed down or tired?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed that happening. When I’m not weighed down or tired, I don’t think I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two years where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s been pretty steady for about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I can’t think of a couple months where I really felt better.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you are feeling your worst?\n\nPatient: When I’m at my worst, I’m usually not pacing around or anything like that. I mostly just feel stuck, like I’m sitting there and it’s hard to move. I haven’t really noticed fidgeting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. When I’m anxious or upset, I can feel tense, but I haven’t really noticed myself fidgeting a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… like I said, not really. I get tense or worked up inside, but I don’t usually notice myself fidgeting or moving around a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can feel tense, but I don’t usually have trouble sitting still or feel like I’m fidgeting much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having trouble sitting still when you're feeling anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed that, no. When I’m anxious or upset I feel it more inside, but I don’t think I’m pacing around or unable to sit still."
